Let us consider the second case
\mathcal{A}=M_{2}\oplus M_{2}
and
\mathcal{B}=M_{4}
. The minimum values for the Higgs potential in Fig.
show a line of discontinuity that have a counterpart in the mass spectrum in Fig.
. Exploring
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}\in[-1,3]
in Fig.
14.7
shows that there are other discontinuities (at least one in the range considered) as in Fig.
14.2
. In the mass spectrum in Fig.
14.7b
, the
a^{1}
and
a^{2}
-lines have degeneracy
3
, the
b
-lines have degeneracy
8
, and the
d
-lines have degeneracy
1
. For
i=1,2
, the slope of the
a^{i}_{1}
and
a^{i}_{3}
(resp.
b_{1}
and
b_{3}
) straight lines is
2=\sqrt{2n_{1}}=\sqrt{2n_{2}}
(resp.
\sqrt{3}
). As in the previous case, modulo very small off-diagonal values in the mass matrix, the
6=2\times 3
fields in the
a^{i}
-lines are inherited from the
2\times 3
fields
A^{1,\kappa}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\mu}
and
A^{2,\kappa}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\mu}
from the two copies of
M_{2}
and the
d
-lines correspond to the diagonal direction
\diag(1,1,-1,-1)/\sqrt{4}\in M_{4}
. The plot in Fig.
14.10a
shows how the distribution of these gauge fields changes along the anti-diagonal
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}=0.5
. The perfect symmetry around the diagonal at
\lambda_{1}=0.25
in Fig.
14.10a
shows that the two
M_{2}
blocks play an equal role, as expected. At
\lambda_{1}=0.5
, we end up on the side
\lambda_{2}=0
in Fig.
, where the top line (end of the
a^{1}
-line) has a slope
2=\sqrt{2n_{1}}
with respect to
\lambda_{1}\in[0,1]
; the middle line has a slope
\sqrt{3/2}
(the
b
-line); and the lower line has a slope
0
(it corresponds to the ends of the
a^{2}
-line and the
e
-line). Here again, at least in the region
\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}\leq 0.5
(before the first discontinuity), we have checked numerically that the masses of the inherited fields are preserved by the map
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
.

The third case
\mathcal{A}=M_{2}\oplus M_{2}
and
\mathcal{B}=M_{5}
, illustrated in Figs.
14.5
,
14.8
, and
14.10b
, differs from the previous one by the greater number of new degrees of freedom in
\mathcal{B}
. The discontinuity in Figs.
and
is larger. Its position has also moved, as can be seen also in Fig.
14.8b
. In this latter plot, the
a^{1}
and
a^{2}
-lines have degeneracy
3
, the
b
-line have degeneracy
8
, the
c^{1}
and
c^{2}
-lines have degeneracy
4
, the
d
-lines have degeneracy
1
, and the
e
-lines have degeneracy
1
. Notice that the
d
and
e
lines are almost always merged in the plot, except for
d_{2}
and
e_{2}
which are close but clearly separated. For
i=1,2
, the slope of the
a^{i}_{1}
and
a^{i}_{3}
(resp.
b_{1}
and
b_{3}
, resp.
c^{i}_{1}
and
c^{i}_{3}
) straight lines is
2=\sqrt{2n_{1}}=\sqrt{2n_{2}}
(resp.
\sqrt{3}
, resp.
\sqrt{3/2}
). Modulo very small off-diagonal values in the mass matrix, the
6=2\times 3
fields in the
a^{i}
-lines are inherited from the
2\times 3
fields
A^{1,\kappa}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\mu}
and
A^{2,\kappa}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\mu}
from the two copies of
M_{2}
. In accordance with the nomenclature of the labels, the
8
fields in the
b
-lines are new degrees of freedom along directions
E^{1}_{\!\mathcal{B},\beta}
that are contained in
M_{4}\subset M_{5}
, where
M_{4}
contains the two copies of
M_{2}
. The
8=2\times 4
fields in the
c^{i}
-lines are new degrees of freedom along directions
E^{1}_{\!\mathcal{B},\beta}
that are defined with components outside of this
M_{4}\subset M_{5}
: the
c^{1}
-line (resp.
c^{2}
-line) corresponds to fields in the directions
E^{1}_{\!\mathcal{B},\beta}
with non zero entries outside of
M_{4}\subset M_{5}
and in the same rows and same columns as the ones in
M_{n_{1}}
(resp.
M_{n_{2}}
). In other words, the
E^{1}_{\!\mathcal{B},\beta}
for the
c^{1}
-line do not commute with
\phi(M_{n_{1}})
while they commute with
\phi(M_{n_{2}})
, and vice versa for the
c^{2}
-line. The
d
-lines correspond to the diagonal direction
\diag(1,1,-1,-1,0)/\sqrt{4}\in M_{5}
and the
e
-lines correspond to the diagonal direction
\diag(1,1,1,1,-4)/\sqrt{20}\in M_{5}
. The anti-diagonal plot in Fig.
14.10b
brings us more information concerning the relationship between the
a^{i}
and
c^{i}
-lines: it seems that there is a correlation between the
a^{1}
-line (resp.
a^{2}
-line) and the
c^{1}
-line (resp.
c^{2}
-line) due to the fact that their associated directions
E^{1}_{\!\mathcal{B},\beta}
do not commute. This non commutativity could also explain the curved
b
-line which is “constrained” by the directions in the
a^{1}
and
a^{2}
-lines.

Finally, the fourth case
\mathcal{A}=M_{2}\oplus M_{3}
and
\mathcal{B}=M_{5}
, illustrated in Figs.
14.6
,
14.9
, and
14.10c
, is closer to the second case than to the third case. We conjecture that this is due to the fact that the diagonal in
\mathcal{B}
is filled by
\phi
in the second and fourth cases, while there is a remaining
0
in the third case (which permits the existence of the directions for the
c^{1}
and
c^{2}
-lines in Fig.
14.10b
). In the mass spectrum in Fig.
14.9b
, the
a^{1}
-lines have degeneracy
3
, the
a^{2}
-lines have degeneracy
8
, the
b
-lines have degeneracy
12
, and the
d
-lines have degeneracy
1
. The slope of the
a^{1}_{1}
and
a^{1}_{3}
(resp.
a^{2}_{1}
and
a^{2}_{3}
, resp.
b_{1}
and
b_{3}
) straight lines is
2=\sqrt{2n_{1}}
(resp.
\sqrt{6}=\sqrt{2n_{2}}
, resp.
\sqrt{25/6}
). Modulo very small off-diagonal values in the mass matrix, the
3
fields in the
a^{1}
-lines are inherited from the
3
fields
A^{1,\kappa}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\mu}
from
M_{n_{1}}=M_{2}
and the
8
fields in the
a^{2}
-lines are inherited from the
8
fields
A^{2,\kappa}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\mu}
from
M_{n_{2}}=M_{3}
. The
d
-lines correspond to the diagonal direction
\diag(1,1,1,1,-4)/\sqrt{20}\in M_{5}
. As showed in Fig.
14.10c
, the mass spectrum along the anti-diagonal is no more symmetric, as can also be seen in Fig.
14.6b
(look for instance at the singular line in the mass spectrum): this distinguishes this case from the second one and illustrates how a change in the algebra
\mathcal{A}
affects the mass spectrum.

Let us make comments on these results. The exploration of the space of configurations for the fields
B^{i,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\kappa}
’s along paths parametrized by the
\lambda_{i}
’s already shows a rich typology concerning the possible masses for the gauge bosons
A^{k,\beta}_{\mathcal{B},\mu}
.

As seen in Fig.
14.2
for instance, the minimum for a conflictual situation
\lambda_{1}=1
and
\lambda_{2}=0
(conflict between the two minimal configurations for the
B^{1}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\kappa}
and
B^{2}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\kappa}
in
M_{2}
) is non zero and produces a global configuration for the fields
B^{1}_{\!\mathcal{B},\beta}
that is neither the null-configuration nor the basis-configuration. The induced masses show 3 possible values with degeneracies. Inserting this configuration as a initial data for another step into a sequence of NCGFT constructed on the sequence
\{(\mathcal{A}_{n},\phi_{n,m})\,/\,0\leq n<m\}
, may propagate this in-between result and produce more subtle configurations with richer possibilities for the masses of the gauge bosons.

Since the exploration of the space of configurations for the fields
B^{i,\kappa^{\prime}}_{\!\!\mathcal{A},\kappa}
’s is reduced to paths parametrized by the
\lambda_{i}
’s, our results do not offer a general and systematic view of what could happen in our kind of models. Nevertheless, the results presented above already display a rich phenomenology from which some information can be drawn. The first noticeable feature is that the mass spectra, constrained by the
\phi
-compatibility, reveal that the masses are grouped in specific directions, so that we have neither a full degeneracy (as in Fig.
14.1b
) nor a complete list of independent masses (as many masses as degrees of freedom): these specific directions are grouped according to the inherited degrees of freedom (the
a^{i}
-lines), according to the way the new degrees of freedom commute or not with the inherited ones (the
b
and
c^{i}
-lines), and according to the possible new diagonal degrees of freedom one can introduce (the
d
and
e
-lines). Masses for inherited gauge bosons are preserved by the
\phi
-compatibility condition quite systematically near the null-configuration. Concerning the first discontinuity on the diagonal plots before the basis-configuration, the position of this discontinuity seems to be related, by an approximate linear relationship, to the ratio of the number of new degrees of freedom over the number of inherited degrees of freedom, see Table
14.1
:

Chapter 15
Spectral Triple-based Approach to NCGFT on
AF
-Algebras

In this chapter, we propose to set up NCGFT based on
AF
-Algebras using the spectral triple-based approach. In subsection
15.1.2
, we will see how to lift arrows of a Bratteli diagram to arrows between Krajewski diagrams. In subsection
15.2.1
we will create the “
\phi
-normalized” map for physical purposes. In subsection
15.2.2
, the spectral actions defining NCGFTs associated with two spectral triples related by the arrows of the Bratteli diagram are compared (tensored by a commutative spectral triple to put us in the context of Almost Commutative manifolds). This is done by using the
\phi
-normalized map and working with
\phi
-compatible operators at the level of the two successive spectral triples of the elementary step in the inductive sequence. This chapter is an account of the results given in
(
Masson and Nieuviarts, 2022
)
. Other works consisting in the development of spectral triples and non-commutative gauge theories on AF algebras in a different way can be found in
(
Christensen and Ivan, 2006
;
Marcolli and van Suijlekom, 2014
)
.

15.1
Lifting one Step of the Defining Inductive Sequence

In this section, we adapt to spectral triples the inclusion map
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
. As explained in Chapt.
13
, the main idea, which is central in this thesis, is to define a notion of
\phi
-compatibility for the structures defining spectral triples
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}},\gamma_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}},\gamma_{\mathcal{B}})
on top of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
. This construction, applied in Sect.
13
to
AF
-algebras, can be interpreted as a lift of arrows in a Bratteli diagram to arrows between Krajewski diagrams.

15.1.1
General Situations

In this subsection, we will explore the consequences of
\phi
-compatibility and strong
\phi
-compatibility conditions on the structure of spectral triples.

Definition 15.1.1
(
\phi
-compatibility of spectral triples).

Assume given a
\phi
-compatible map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Two odd spectral triples
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}})
are said to be
\phi
-compatible if
D_{\mathcal{A}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Two real spectral triples
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}})
are said to be
\phi
-compatible if
D_{\mathcal{A}}
(resp.
J_{\mathcal{A}}
) is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{B}}
(resp.
J_{\mathcal{B}}
).

In the even case for
\mathcal{A}
, one requires that
\mathcal{B}
is also even and that the grading operators
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
are
\phi
-compatible.

Strong
\phi
-compatibility of spectral triples can be defined in an obvious way.

Remark 15.1.2
.

Notice that strong
\phi
-compatibility of spectral triples is similar to the condition (3) given in
(
Floricel and Ghorbanpour, 2019
, Def 2.1)
where their couple
(\phi,I)
corresponds to our couple
(\phi,\phi_{\mathcal{H}})
. We depart from this paper where inductive sequences of spectral triples are studied in the following way: we will restrict our analysis to the algebraic part of spectral triples since only
AF
-algebras will be considered later, so that the analytic part is quite trivial in our situation, and we will focus on gauge fields theories defined on top of spectral triples. For instance, conditions like (ST1) (about the
*
-subalgebra
\mathcal{A}^{\infty}
) and (ST2) (about the compactness of the resolvent of the Dirac operator) in
(
Floricel and Ghorbanpour, 2019
)
will not be considered here. Moreover, since we are interested in accumulating “new degrees of freedom” along the inductive limit, the
\phi
-compatibility condition will be more effective than the strong
\phi
-compatibility condition.

Since
J_{\mathcal{A}}
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}
define
\mathcal{A}^{e}=\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A}^{\circ}
and
\mathcal{B}^{e}=\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{\circ}
modules structures on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
, it is convenient to express
\phi
-compatibility in terms of this structure. The morphism
\phi
defines a canonical morphism of algebras
\phi^{\circ}:\mathcal{A}^{\circ}\to\mathcal{B}^{\circ}
by the relation
\phi^{\circ}(a^{\circ})\vcentcolon=\phi(a)^{\circ}
. We then define
\phi^{e}:\mathcal{A}^{e}\to\mathcal{B}^{e}
as
\phi^{e}\vcentcolon=\phi\otimes\phi^{\circ}
,
i.e.

\phi^{e}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}^{\circ})=\phi(a_{1})\otimes\phi^{\circ}(a_{2}^{\circ})
. Let
\mathcal{M}
(resp.
\mathcal{N}
) be a
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule (resp.
\mathcal{B}
-bimodule), which is also a
\mathcal{A}^{e}
-left module ( resp.
\mathcal{B}^{e}
-left module) by
(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}^{\circ})e\vcentcolon=a_{1}ea_{2}
for any
e\in\mathcal{M}
and
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathcal{A}
(and similar relations for
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{N}
). Then, we say that a linear map between the two bimodules
\phi_{\text{{Mod}}}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
is
\phi
-compatible if it is
\phi^{e}
-compatible between the two left modules, that is
\phi_{\text{{Mod}}}((a_{1}\otimes a_{2}^{\circ})e)=\phi^{e}(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}^{\circ})\phi_{\text{{Mod}}}(e)
, which is equivalent to
\phi_{\text{{Mod}}}(a_{1}ea_{2})=\phi(a_{1})\phi_{\text{{Mod}}}(e)\phi(a_{2})
.

Lemma 15.1.3
.

Suppose that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible as a map of left modules and that
J_{\mathcal{A}}
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}
are strong
\phi
-compatible. Then
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is
\phi^{e}
-compatible as a map between the bimodules defined by the real operators.

Proof

For any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
,
a_{1},a_{2}\in\mathcal{A}
, by definition, one has
a_{1}\psi a_{2}=(a_{1}\otimes a_{2}^{\circ})\psi=a_{1}J_{\mathcal{A}}a_{2}^{\ast}J_{\mathcal{A}}\psi
. On the one hand, since
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is
\phi
-compatible, one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(a_{1}\psi)=\phi(a_{1})\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
. On the other hand,
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi a_{2})=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(J_{\mathcal{A}}a_{2}^{\ast}J_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)=J_{\mathcal{B}}\phi(a_{2})^{\ast}J_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\phi(a_{2})
.

Lemma 15.1.4
.

Suppose that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
:

1.

\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}
.

2.

J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}

3.

J_{\mathcal{B}}
is diagonal in its matrix decomposition.

4.

If two operators
A
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
B
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
are
\phi
-compatible, then the operators
J_{\mathcal{A}}AJ_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}BJ_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
are
\phi
-compatible.

Proof

From
J_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}
, one gets
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(J_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}\psi)=J_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, so that
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}
. From this we deduce that
J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}J_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Let
J_{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}&J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}\\
J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}&J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. Since
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
, we already know that
J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}=0
. Let
\psi_{\mathcal{B}}\in\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}})
and
\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}}\in\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
. Then
J_{\mathcal{B}}(\psi_{\mathcal{B}})=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}(\psi_{\mathcal{B}})\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})\\
J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, so that
0=\langle\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}},\psi_{\mathcal{B}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle J_{\mathcal{B}}(\psi_{\mathcal{B}}),J_{\mathcal{B}}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}(\psi_{\mathcal{B}}),J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}
. From
J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B}}
and
J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}=0
, one gets that
J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}
is invertible with
(J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi})^{-1}=(J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1})_{\phi}^{\phi}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}
, so that
J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}(\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}))=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
, which implies that
J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})\in\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
, that is,
J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}(\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}})=0
for any
\psi^{\prime}_{\mathcal{B}}\in\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
, and so
J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}=0
.

From
(J_{\mathcal{B}}BJ_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1})_{\phi}^{\phi}=J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}B_{\phi}^{\phi}(J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi})^{-1}
, we deduce that the operators
J_{\mathcal{A}}AJ_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}BJ_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
are
\phi
-compatible.

Lemma 15.1.5
.

Let us consider the even case and suppose
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
.

1.

Then
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is diagonal in its matrix decomposition, so that strong
\phi
-compatibility and
\phi
-compatibility between
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
are equivalent.

2.

Then
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is diagonal for the matrix decomposition induced by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}^{+}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}^{-}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{+}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{-}
, so that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
restricts to maps
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\pm}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\pm}
.

Proof

Point
1
: since
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{\dagger}=\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
, one has
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}&\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}\\
\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp\dagger}&\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
. The
\phi
-compatibility implies
(\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi})^{2}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
, so that
(\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi})^{2}=1
. Since
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{2}=1
, one has
(\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi})^{2}+\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp\dagger}=1
, from which we get
\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp\dagger}=0
, which implies
\gamma_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}=0
, so that
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is diagonal. By Prop.
13.4.5
, this implies strong
\phi
-compatibility.

Point
2
: for every
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}^{\pm}
, one has
\pm\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)=\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
, so that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}^{\pm}
.

Proposition 15.1.6
.

A

1.

If two (odd/even) real spectral triples are strong
\phi
-compatible, then they have the same
KO
-dimension (mod 8).

2.

If two (odd/even) real spectral triples are
\phi
-compatible and
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
, then they have the same
KO
-dimension (mod 8).

Proof

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}})
be two strong
\phi
-compatible real spectral triple. In the even case, consider the gradings
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
. Then one has
J_{\mathcal{A}}^{2}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}
,
J_{\mathcal{A}}D_{\mathcal{A}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}D_{\mathcal{A}}J_{\mathcal{A}}
, and
J_{\mathcal{A}}\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}J_{\mathcal{A}}
(in the even case), and similar relations for
\mathcal{B}
. We already know from Lemma
15.1.4
that
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}
. For any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(J_{\mathcal{A}}D_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)=J_{\mathcal{B}}D_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(D_{\mathcal{A}}J_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)=D_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
, so that
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
, and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(J_{\mathcal{A}}\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)=J_{\mathcal{B}}\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}J_{\mathcal{A}}\psi)=\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
, so that
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime\prime}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}
.

The second assertion follows the same line of computations, using the fact that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
are diagonal (Lemmas
15.1.4
and
15.1.5
), so that in particular
(J_{\mathcal{B}}D_{\mathcal{B}})_{\phi}^{\phi}=J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}
and
(D_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B}})_{\phi}^{\phi}=D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}
.

The requirement that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
be strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
seems to be inevitable in the generic situation to get the same
KO
-dimension. In the case of
AF
-algebras, this requirement will be a consequence of another requirement on the
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
map, see Prop.
15.1.15
.

Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})
be two unitary equivalent real spectral triples for
{U_{\mathcal{A}}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
and let
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}})
and
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}})
be two unitary equivalent real spectral triples for
{U_{\mathcal{B}}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
and
\phi_{\mathcal{B}}:\mathcal{B}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
.

Proposition 15.1.7
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}})
are strong
\phi
-compatible, and that there are a morphism of algebra
\phi^{\prime}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and a linear map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
such that
\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{A}}=\phi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}({U_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi)={U_{\mathcal{B}}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
. Then
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})
and
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}})
are strong
\phi^{\prime}
-compatible. If the spectral triples are even, the result holds also.

This result shows that strong
\phi
-compatibility is transported by unitary equivalence if one assumes some natural conditions on the maps
\phi^{\prime}
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}
, which are the commutativity of the following diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\prime}}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathcal{H}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U_{\mathcal{B}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}}

Proof

For any
\psi^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
, let
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
be the unique vector such that
\psi^{\prime}={U_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi
, and for any
a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
, let
a\in\mathcal{A}
the unique element such that
a^{\prime}=\phi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)
. Then one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})\psi^{\prime})=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}((\pi_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)){U_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}({U_{\mathcal{A}}}\pi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)\psi)={U_{\mathcal{B}}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\pi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)\psi)={U_{\mathcal{B}}}(\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi(a))\phi_{\mathcal{H}}({U_{\mathcal{A}}}^{-1}\psi^{\prime})={U_{\mathcal{B}}}(\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi(a)){U_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime})=(\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi(a))\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime})=(\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\phi^{\prime}(a^{\prime}))\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime})
, so that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}
is
\phi^{\prime}
-compatible. Let
A
and
B
be strong
\phi
-compatible operators on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
and define
A^{\prime}\vcentcolon={U_{\mathcal{A}}}A{U_{\mathcal{A}}}^{-1}
and
B^{\prime}\vcentcolon={U_{\mathcal{B}}}B{U_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
. Then one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(A^{\prime}\psi^{\prime})=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}({U_{\mathcal{A}}}A\psi)={U_{\mathcal{B}}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(A\psi)={U_{\mathcal{B}}}B\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=B^{\prime}{U_{\mathcal{B}}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=B^{\prime}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}({U_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi)=B^{\prime}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\psi^{\prime})
, so that
A^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime}
are strong
\phi^{\prime}
-compatible. Applying this result to
D_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
and
D_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
(resp.
J_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
and
J_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
, resp.
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}
and
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
in the even case) shows that
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})
and
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}})
are strong
\phi^{\prime}
-compatible and similarly in the even case.

In the proof, the commutativity of the first diagram is only used when the representation
\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
is applied, and more specifically, when this representation acts on
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})
. In other words, the minimal condition in this proof is that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{A}}=\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi
holds as operators acting on
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})\subset\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
.

Another version using only
\phi
-compatibility can be proposed in the following way:

Proposition 15.1.8
.

Suppose that
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}})
are
\phi
-compatible, and that there is a morphism of algebra
\phi^{\prime}:\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and a linear map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
such that
\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{A}}=\phi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}({U_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi)={U_{\mathcal{B}}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and suppose that
{U_{\mathcal{B}}}
is diagonal. Then
(\mathcal{A}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{A}^{\prime}})
and
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},D_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}},J_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}})
are
\phi^{\prime}
-compatible. If the spectral triples are even, the result holds also.

Proof

The proof follows the same line of reasoning as for the proof of Prop.
15.1.7
, with the key difference that, from
B^{\prime}\vcentcolon={U_{\mathcal{B}}}B{U_{\mathcal{B}}}^{-1}
and the fact that
{U_{\mathcal{B}}}
is diagonal, one has
B^{\prime\phi}_{\phi}={U_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\phi}_{\phi}B^{\phi}_{\phi}({U_{\mathcal{B}}}^{\phi}_{\phi})^{-1}
.

The map
\phi
induces a natural map of graded algebras
\phi:\mathcal{T}^{\bullet}\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{T}^{\bullet}\mathcal{B}
by the relation
\phi(a^{0}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{n})=\phi(a^{0})\otimes\cdots\otimes\phi(a^{n})
. If
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
, then one can check that
\phi(\omega)\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\mathcal{B})
, so that
\phi
restricts to a map of graded algebras
\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}(\mathcal{A})\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{U}(\mathcal{B})
. If
\phi({\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{A}})={\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{B}}
, then
\phi(\text{{d}}_{U}a)=\phi({\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{A}}\otimes a-a\otimes{\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{A}})={\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{B}}\otimes\phi(a)-\phi(a)\otimes{\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{B}}=\text{{d}}_{U}\phi(b)
. If
\phi({\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{A}})\neq{\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{B}}
, let
p_{\phi}\vcentcolon=\phi({\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{A}})\in\mathcal{B}
be the induced projection. Then
\phi(\text{{d}}_{U}a)=p_{\phi}\otimes\phi(a)-\phi(a)\otimes p_{\phi}\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\mathcal{B})
can be written as
\phi(\text{{d}}_{U}a)=p_{\phi}\text{{d}}_{U}\phi(a)+\phi(a)\text{{d}}_{U}({\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{B}}-p_{\phi})=p_{\phi}\text{{d}}_{U}\phi(a)-\phi(a)\text{{d}}_{U}p_{\phi}
. This shows that
\phi
is a morphism of differential algebras only when it is unital. In the following, we will use the most general relation
\phi(a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1})=\phi(a^{0})\text{{d}}_{U}\phi(a^{1})-\phi(a^{0}a^{1})\text{{d}}_{U}p_{\phi}
since
\phi(a)p_{\phi}=\phi(a)
.

Proposition 15.1.9
.

Suppose that
D_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}
.

1.

For any
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
,
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}\circ\phi(\omega)
is
\phi
-compatible with
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{A}}}(\omega)
.

2.

Suppose that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
. For any unitaries
{u_{\mathcal{A}}}\in\mathcal{A}
and
{u_{\mathcal{B}}}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
are
\phi
-compatible and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
is diagonal in the matrix decomposition,
D_{\mathcal{B}}^{{u_{\mathcal{B}}}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}^{{u_{\mathcal{A}}}}
.

3.

Using the hypothesis of the previous points,
D_{\mathcal{B},\phi(\omega)}^{{u_{\mathcal{B}}}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A},\omega}^{{u_{\mathcal{A}}}}
.

Condition
2
in this Proposition implies in particular that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{A}}=\pi_{\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}\circ\phi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi
(see comment after Prop.
15.1.7
) with
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}=\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}=\mathcal{B}
and
\phi^{\prime}=\phi
.

Proof

We can reduce the general case to
\omega=a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1}\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
. Let us then consider
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}\circ\phi(a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1})=\phi(a^{0})[D_{\mathcal{B}},\phi(a^{1})]-\phi(a^{0}a^{1})[D_{\mathcal{B}},p_{\phi}]
(with
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}
omitted in this relation and the following). For any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, one has
\phi(a^{0}a^{1})[D_{\mathcal{B}},p_{\phi}]\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi(a^{0}a^{1})D_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)-\phi(a^{0}a^{1})\phi({\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\mathcal{A}})D_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=0
, since
p_{\phi}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
, so that
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}\circ\phi(a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1})\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi(a^{0})[D_{\mathcal{B}},\phi(a^{1})]\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
. Using the matrix decomposition
D_{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}&D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}\\
D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}&D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and Point
8
in Prop.
13.4.5
, one gets

\displaystyle\phi(a^{0})[D_{\mathcal{B}},\phi(a^{1})]\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\\
0\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\phi(a^{0})_{\phi}^{\phi}[D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi},\phi(a^{1})_{\phi}^{\phi}]\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\\
\phi(a^{0})_{\perp}^{\perp}(D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}\phi(a^{1})_{\phi}^{\phi}-\phi(a^{1})_{\perp}^{\perp}D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi})\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\end{pmatrix}

From this relation we get
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}(\phi(a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1}))_{\phi}^{\phi}=\phi(a^{0})_{\phi}^{\phi}[D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi},\phi(a^{1})_{\phi}^{\phi}]
and then
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}(\phi(a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1}))_{\phi}^{\phi}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi(a^{0})_{\phi}^{\phi}[D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi},\phi(a^{1})_{\phi}^{\phi}]\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(a^{0}[D_{\mathcal{A}},a^{1}]\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\pi_{D_{\mathcal{A}}}(a^{0}\text{{d}}_{U}a^{1})\psi)
since
D_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Using the hypothesis that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
is diagonal, a straightforward computation gives
(\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{B}},\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})])_{\phi}^{\phi}=(\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\dagger})_{\phi}^{\phi}[D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi},\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})_{\phi}^{\phi}]
from which we deduce that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{B}},\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})]
is
\phi
-compatible with
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{A}},\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})]
. From Lemma
15.1.4
, we deduce that
J_{\mathcal{B}}\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{B}},\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})]J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
is
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{A}},\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})]J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
, and so that
D_{\mathcal{B}}^{{u_{\mathcal{B}}}}=D_{\mathcal{B}}+\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{B}},\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})]+\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}J_{\mathcal{B}}\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{B}},\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})]J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
is
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}^{{u_{\mathcal{A}}}}=D_{\mathcal{A}}+\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{A}},\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})]+\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}J_{\mathcal{A}}\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})^{\dagger}[D_{\mathcal{A}},\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})]J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
since
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
by Prop
15.1.6
.

The last point combines the two previous results by replacing
D_{\mathcal{A}}
by
D_{\mathcal{A},\omega}=D_{\mathcal{A}}+\pi_{D_{\mathcal{A}}}(\omega)+\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}J_{\mathcal{A}}\pi_{D_{\mathcal{A}}}(\omega)J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
and
D_{\mathcal{B}}
by
D_{\mathcal{B},\phi(\omega)}=D_{\mathcal{B}}+\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}(\phi(\omega))+\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}J_{\mathcal{B}}\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}(\phi(\omega))J_{\mathcal{B}}^{-1}
which are
\phi
-compatible by the first point, Lemma
15.1.4
, and Prop
15.1.6
.

Notice that one can associate to any unitary
{u_{\mathcal{A}}}\in\mathcal{A}
the diagonal (unitary) operator
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi({u_{\mathcal{A}}})&0\\
0&{\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
where
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi({u_{\mathcal{A}}})\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\vcentcolon=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})\psi)
for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
{\text{\char 49\relax}}_{\perp}^{\perp}
is the identity operator on
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
. But this operator is not necessarily of the form
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
for a unitary
{u_{\mathcal{B}}}\in\mathcal{B}
. In the case of
AF
-algebras, it will be possible to construct a unitary
{u_{\mathcal{B}}}\in\mathcal{B}
from
{u_{\mathcal{A}}}
such that
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
are (strong)
\phi
-compatible and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
is diagonal, see Prop.
15.1.14
.

A strong version of the previous proposition can be proposed, for which a proof is not necessary since it combines previous results and the same line of reasoning when computations are needed:

Proposition 15.1.10
.

Suppose that
D_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}
.

1.

For any
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\mathcal{A})
,
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{B}}}\circ\phi(\omega)
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
\pi_{D_{\mathcal{A}}}(\omega)
.

2.

Suppose that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
. For any unitaries
{u_{\mathcal{A}}}\in\mathcal{A}
and
{u_{\mathcal{B}}}\in\mathcal{B}
such that
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
are strong
\phi
-compatible,
D_{\mathcal{B}}^{{u_{\mathcal{B}}}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}^{{u_{\mathcal{A}}}}
.

3.

Using the hypothesis of the previous points,
D_{\mathcal{B},\phi(\omega)}^{{u_{\mathcal{B}}}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A},\omega}^{{u_{\mathcal{A}}}}
.

15.1.2
AF
-Algebras in terms of Krajewski’s Diagram Structures

We will use the results of section
4.2
, in particular the diagrammatic descriptions of (odd/even) real spectral triples. Let
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}})
be two real spectral triples on the algebras
\mathcal{A}=\toplus_{i=1}^{r}M_{n_{i}}
and
\mathcal{B}=\toplus_{k=1}^{s}M_{m_{k}}
with
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}=\toplus_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}=\toplus_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
. As we defined the maps
i,j
on
\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
, let us define the similar maps
k,\ell
on
\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
: for any
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
with
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(w)=({\mathbf{m}}_{k},{\mathbf{m}}_{\ell})
,
k(w)\vcentcolon=k
and
\ell(w)\vcentcolon=\ell
.

Let
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
be a
\phi
-compatible linear map of bimodules (
\phi^{e}
-compatible as left modules). This map decomposes along the maps
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
between irreps on both sides. For any
a=\toplus_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
,
b=\toplus_{i=1}^{r}b_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
, and
\psi=\toplus_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\psi_{v}
, one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(ab^{\circ}\psi)=\phi(a)\phi(b)^{\circ}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
with (using (
4.2.3
))

\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(ab^{\circ}\psi)=\sum_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\toplus_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(a_{i(v)}b_{j(v)}^{\circ}\psi_{v})
and
\phi(a)\phi(b)^{\circ}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\sum_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\toplus_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\phi(a)_{k(w)}\phi(b)_{\ell(w)}^{\circ}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})
.

We can select a fixed
v
and choose
\psi
with only one non-zero component
\psi_{v}
. Then one gets, for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and any
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
,
\phi_{\mathcal{H},v}^{w}(a_{i(v)}b_{j(v)}^{\circ}\psi_{v})=\phi(a)_{k(w)}\phi(a)_{\ell(w)}^{\circ}\phi_{\mathcal{H},v}^{w}(\psi_{v})
. Let us now consider a fixed index
i
and
a
with only non-zero component at
i(v)=i
, and the same for a fixed
j
and
b
: with
(k,\ell)=(k(w),\ell(w))
, one has

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(a_{i}b_{j}^{\circ}\psi_{v})=\phi_{k}^{i}(a_{i})\phi_{\ell}^{j}(b_{j})^{\circ}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})

(15.1.1)

This relation, combined with (
13.2.3
), suggests to decompose
\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}
in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}=\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}
as
\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}=[\toplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}]\oplus\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,k}}
and similarly for
\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}
with a last term
\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,\ell}}
, so that one has the orthogonal decomposition

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}=\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}=

\displaystyle[\toplus_{i,j=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}]\oplus[\toplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,\ell}\circ}]

(15.1.2)

\displaystyle\oplus[\toplus_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}]\oplus[\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,\ell}\circ}]

.

(15.1.3)

For any
i,j=1,\dots,r
and
k,\ell=1,\dots,s
, let us define the inclusion

\displaystyle I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}:\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}.

Notice that
I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}=I_{k}^{i}\otimes I_{\ell}^{j\circ}
with the inclusions
I_{k}^{i}:\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}
and
I_{\ell}^{j\circ}:\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}
.

Let
F^{(i,k),(j,\ell)}_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\to\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}\circ}
be the involution
\xi_{i}\otimes\sigma^{k}_{i}\otimes\sigma^{\ell}_{j}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\mapsto\eta_{j}\otimes\sigma^{\ell}_{j}\otimes\sigma^{k}_{i}\otimes\xi_{i}^{\circ}
and
F^{k\ell}_{\mathcal{B}}:\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}\to\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}\circ}
the involution
\varphi_{k}\otimes\vartheta_{\ell}^{\circ}\mapsto\vartheta_{\ell}\otimes\varphi_{k}^{\circ}
. Then, one can check that

\displaystyle F^{k\ell}_{\mathcal{B}}\circ I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}

\displaystyle=I_{\ell,k}^{j,i}\circ F^{(i,k),(j,\ell)}_{\mathcal{A}}

(15.1.4)

Notice that
J_{\mathcal{A}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}
can be written as
J_{\mathcal{A}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(v,d_{\mathcal{A}})(J_{0}\otimes J_{0})\circ F^{ij}_{\mathcal{A}}
since
F^{ij}_{\mathcal{A}}=\widehat{\kappa}_{\mathcal{A},v}
with
(i,j)=(i(v),j(v))
.

In the case of a
AF
-algebra, the inclusions
I_{k}^{i}
(and so
I_{\ell}^{j\circ}
and
I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}
) are defined directly from the Bratteli diagram of the algebra, that is, they depend only on the inclusion
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
. We can now write
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
in terms of these inclusions.

Combining (
15.1.1
) and (
15.1.2
), the map
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
first reduces to a map
\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\to\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}
, and using a slight adaptation of Lemma
4.2.1
, it reduces to a linear map
\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}
, that is, to the data of an element
u(v,w)\in\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}
, such that
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
is the composition of
\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\ni\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\mapsto\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\in\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}
with the inclusion
I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}
. When
\alpha_{ki}=0
or
\alpha_{\ell j}=0
,
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}=0
.

Consequently, the
\phi
-compatible map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is completely determined by a family of matrices
u(v,w)\in M_{\alpha_{ki}\times\alpha_{\ell j}}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}
1
1

1

We use the following convention. Let
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}^{m}
. Define
\mathbb{C}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m}\ni x\otimes y\simeq z=xy^{\top}\in M_{n\times m}
and
z^{\prime}=x^{\prime}y^{\prime\top}\in M_{n\times m}
, so that
z(v)=\langle\bar{y},v\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}x
for any
v\in\mathbb{C}^{m}
. One then gets
\bar{y}\otimes\bar{x}\simeq z^{\ast}
and
\tr(z^{\ast}z^{\prime})=\langle x,x^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n}}\langle y,y^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{m}}
, and by linearity, a similar relation holds for
z=\sum_{i}x_{i}y_{i}^{\top}
and
z^{\prime}=\sum_{i}x^{\prime}_{i}y_{i}^{\prime\top}
. This relation will be used in the following.

by the previous relation, with
(i,j)=(i(v),j(v))
and
(k,\ell)=(k(w),\ell(w))
. Notice that for
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
such that
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
, the ranges of
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v^{\prime}}
are at the same place in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
(the range of
I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}
), and
u(v,w)
and
u(v^{\prime},w)
define a kind of relative positioning and weight between the two ranges. If
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)\neq\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
, the ranges are orthogonal in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
since the ranges of
I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}
and
I_{k,\ell}^{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}
are distinct in the orthogonal decomposition (
15.1.2
) when
(i,j)\neq(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
.

For non-real spectral triples, a similar (simpler) result can be obtained: a
\phi
-compatible map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
is completely determined by the linear maps
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}=\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}=\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}
for
i=i(v)
and
k=k(w)
, and so by a family of vectors
u(v,w)\in\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}
such that
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
is the composition of
\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\ni\xi_{i}\mapsto\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\in\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}
with the inclusion
I_{k}^{i}:\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}
.

The following result summarizes the construction achieved so far:

Lemma 15.1.11
.

There is a family of matrices
u(v,w)\in M_{\alpha_{ki}\times\alpha_{\ell j}}
such that, for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, with
(i,j)=(i(v),j(v))
and
(k,\ell)=(k(w),\ell(w))
, one has

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}(\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ})\quad\text{for any $\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}$.}

(15.1.5)

For any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
, any
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and any
a=\toplus_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
, one has

\displaystyle\phi_{k}^{i}(a_{i})I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}(\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}(a_{i}\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ})

with
(i,j)=(i(v),j(v))
and
(k,\ell)=(k(w),\ell(w))
.

In the even case, if
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
, then
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
, and so
u(v,w)
, can be non-zero only when
s(v)=s(w)
.

Proof

The first statement is a summary of the previous decomposition of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
. The second relation is a direct consequence of this decomposition of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
and (
15.1.1
). Notice that in the LHS, one could replace
\phi_{k}^{i}(a_{i})
by
\phi_{k}(a)
since only the entries positioned according to
i
in the matrix
\phi_{k}(a)\in M_{m_{k}}
, see (
13.2.2
) and (
13.2.3
), give non-zero contributions once applied on the range of
I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}
. In the even case, the statement is a consequence of Lemma
15.1.5
, which implies here that
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}=0
when
s(v)\neq s(w)
.

Let us stress that
u(v,w)
is an essential object since it encodes all the data of embedding (
i.e.

\alpha_{ki}
’s and the way of embedding into these corresponding blocks) which is at the heart of the Bratteli diagram structure. Then, it allows us to link Bratteli’s and Krajewski’s diagrammatic structures since the way its basic elements (nodes
v
and
w
) are now connected to Bratteli’s diagram structures.

Proposition 15.1.12
.

Two operators
A
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
B
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
are strong
\phi
-compatible if and only if

\displaystyle\tsum_{v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(A_{v_{2}}^{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\tsum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}B_{w_{2}}^{w_{1}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{1}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})

(15.1.6)

for any
v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
,
w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and
\psi_{v_{1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v_{1}}
. They are
\phi
-compatible if and only if

\displaystyle\tsum_{v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(A_{v_{2}}^{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\tsum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}B_{\phi,w_{2}}^{\phi,w_{1}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{1}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})

for any
v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
,
w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and
\psi_{v_{1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v_{1}}
, where
B_{\phi,w_{2}}^{\phi,w_{1}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w_{1}}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w_{2}}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
is the decomposition of
B_{\phi}^{\phi}
along the
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
’s.

Proof

For any
\psi=\toplus_{v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\psi_{v_{1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, one has
A\psi=\toplus_{v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\sum_{v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}A_{v_{2}}^{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}}
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\toplus_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\sum_{v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{1}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})
. This implies
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(A\psi)=\toplus_{w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\sum_{v_{1},v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(A_{v_{2}}^{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}})
and, in a similar way,
B\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\toplus_{w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\sum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\sum_{v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}B_{w_{1}}^{w_{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})
. The strong
\phi
-compatibility is then equivalent to
\sum_{v_{1},v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(A_{v_{2}}^{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}})=\sum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\sum_{v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}B_{w_{1}}^{w_{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})
for any
w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and, by linearity (fixing
\psi
with one non-zero component at
v_{1}
),
\sum_{v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(A_{v_{2}}^{v_{1}}\psi_{v_{1}})=\sum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}B_{w_{1}}^{w_{2}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})
for any
v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
.

The
\phi
-compatibility relation follows the same computation with
B
replaced by
B_{\phi}^{\phi}
.

Lemma 15.1.13
.

For any
a=\toplus_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
and
b=\toplus_{k=1}^{s}b_{i}\in\mathcal{B}
,
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)
and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(b)
are strong
\phi
-compatible if and only if, for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
, any
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and any
\xi_{i(v)}\otimes\eta_{j(v)}^{\circ}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
, one has

\displaystyle b_{k(w)}I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}(\xi_{i(v)}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j(v)}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}(a_{i(v)}\xi_{i(v)}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j(v)}^{\circ})

Proof

Inserting (
4.2.3
) into (
15.1.6
) for
A=\pi_{\mathcal{A}}(a)
and
B=\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(b)
, one gets
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(a_{i(v)}\psi_{v})=b_{k(w)}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})
for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
. Using (
15.1.5
) for
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
then gives the relation.

Proposition 15.1.14
.

Let
{u_{\mathcal{A}}}\in\mathcal{A}
be a unitary element and define
{u_{\mathcal{B}}}\vcentcolon=\phi({u_{\mathcal{A}}})+p_{n_{0}}\in\mathcal{B}
(see (
13.2.6
)). Then
{u_{\mathcal{B}}}
is a unitary element such that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
is diagonal (in the orthogonal decomposition defined by
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
) and is strong
\phi
-compatible with
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
.

Proof

One already knows that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}\circ\phi({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
(see Remark
13.4.4
). By construction, the range of
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}
is contained only in the first term in brackets (the double direct sum over
i,j
) in (
15.1.2
), while
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(p_{n_{0}})
is non-trivial only on the last two terms (the ones with
\mathbb{C}^{n_{0,k}}
as first factor). This implies that
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}(p_{n_{0}})\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=0=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi)
for any any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
.
So, one has
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}}\psi)
for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and since
\pi_{\mathcal{A}}({u_{\mathcal{A}}})
and
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
are unitary, by Prop.
13.4.5
,
\pi_{\mathcal{B}}({u_{\mathcal{B}}})
is diagonal.

Proposition 15.1.15
.

J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
if and only if

\displaystyle u(\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v),\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w))

\displaystyle=\frac{\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(v,d_{\mathcal{A}})}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}(w,d_{\mathcal{B}})}u(v,w)^{\ast}

(15.1.7)

for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
where
d_{\mathcal{A}}
(resp.
d_{\mathcal{B}}
) is the
KO
-dimension of
\mathcal{A}
(resp.
\mathcal{B})
.

Prop.
15.1.20
below gives a criterion on spectral triples on top of
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
so that
d_{\mathcal{A}}=d_{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proof

For any
\psi_{v}=\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
, one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H},\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w)}^{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}(J_{\mathcal{A}}\psi_{v})=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(v,d_{\mathcal{A}})\phi_{\mathcal{H},\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w)}^{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}(\bar{\eta}_{j}\otimes\bar{\xi}_{i}^{\circ})=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(v,d_{\mathcal{A}})I_{\ell,k}^{j,i}\big(\bar{\eta}_{j}\otimes u(\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v),\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w))\otimes\bar{\xi}_{i}^{\circ}\big)
and
J_{\mathcal{B}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})=J_{\mathcal{B}}\circ I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}(\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ})=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}(w,d_{\mathcal{B}})I_{\ell,k}^{j,i}\big(\bar{\eta}_{j}\otimes u(v,w)^{\ast}\otimes\bar{\xi}_{i}^{\circ}\big)
when one uses (
15.1.4
) and the identification of
M_{\alpha_{ki}\times\alpha_{\ell j}}
with
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}
(see Footnote
1
). This implies the required equivalence.

Corollary 15.1.16
.

If
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
, then, for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
,
\phi_{\mathcal{H},\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w)}^{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}\neq 0
if and only if
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}\neq 0
.

Proof

For any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, with
(i,j)=(i(v),j(v))
and
(k,\ell)=(k(w),\ell(w))
, from (
15.1.7
), one has

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{H},\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w)}^{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}(\xi_{j}\otimes\eta_{i}^{\circ})

\displaystyle=I_{\ell,k}^{j,i}(\xi_{j}\otimes u(\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v),\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w))\otimes\eta_{i}^{\circ})=\frac{\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(v,d_{\mathcal{A}})}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}(w,d_{\mathcal{B}})}I_{\ell,k}^{j,i}(\xi_{j}\otimes u(v,w)^{\ast}\otimes\eta_{i}^{\circ}).

We then get the equivalence since
u(v,w)
defines
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
.

(a)
A Bratteli diagram

(b)
A lift of a Bratteli diagram between two Krajewski diagrams

Fig. 15.1:
Lift of a Bratteli diagram between two Krajewski diagrams.

: An example of a Bratteli diagram for the inclusion
M_{n_{1}}\oplus M_{n_{2}}\oplus M_{n_{3}}\to M_{m_{1}}\oplus M_{m_{2}}
with multiplicities
\alpha_{ki}
for the inclusion of
M_{n_{i}}
into
M_{m_{k}}
.

: Some lifts of the maps (arrows) given in the Bratteli diagram
as maps
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
, here represented as (green) arrows decorated with their defining matrices
u(v,w)\in\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}
, see (
15.1.5
). The configuration for the arrows
v_{2}\to w_{2}
and
\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v_{2})\to\kappa_{\mathcal{B}}(w_{2})
is the consequence of Corollary
15.1.16
. In the even case, according to Lemma
15.1.11
, one should have
s(v_{2})=s(w_{2})
for
u(v_{2},w_{2})
to be non-zero, and similarly for other arrows. The arrows
M_{n_{2}}\to M_{m_{1}}
and
M_{n_{2}}\to M_{m_{2}}
in
are not lifted in order to lighten the drawing.

For any
v^{\prime}=(i,p^{\prime},j)\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A},\mathbf{n}_{i}\mathbf{n}_{j}}^{(0)}
, we define

\displaystyle\iota_{v}^{v^{\prime}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}\qquad\text{as}\qquad\iota_{v}^{v^{\prime}}(\xi_{i,\alpha}^{(1)}\otimes\sigma_{ij}^{p^{\prime}}\otimes\eta_{j,\alpha}^{\circ(2)})=\xi_{i,\alpha}^{(1)}\otimes\sigma_{ij}^{p}\otimes\eta_{j,\alpha}^{\circ(2)}.

Proposition 15.1.17
.

Let
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
,
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and
\psi_{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v^{\prime}}
.

When
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)\neq\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
, one has
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}}=0
.

When
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
, one has

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}}

\displaystyle=\langle\psi_{v},\iota_{v}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}\,\tr(u(v,w)^{\ast}u(v^{\prime},w))

In particular, for any
\psi_{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
, one has

\displaystyle\lVert\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}}

\displaystyle=\lVert\psi_{v}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}\lVert u(v,w)\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}

\displaystyle=\langle\psi_{v},\iota_{v}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}\Big(\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\tr(u(v,w)^{\ast}u(v^{\prime},w))\Big)

(15.1.8)

where
\lVert-\rVert_{F}
is the Frobenius norm on matrices, defined as
\lVert A\rVert_{F}^{2}\vcentcolon=\tr(A^{\ast}A)
. This implies that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
is one-to-one if and only if
\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\lVert u(v,w)\rVert^{2}_{F}>0
.

Proof

From a previous remark, the scalar product is zero when
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)\neq\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
. So, suppose
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
. Let
i=i(v)=i(v^{\prime})
and
j=j(v)=j(v^{\prime})
and consider
\psi_{v}=\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}
and
\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}=\xi^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\prime\circ}
, so that
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})=\xi_{i}\otimes u(v,w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}
and
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})=\xi^{\prime}_{i}\otimes u(v^{\prime},w)\otimes\eta_{j}^{\prime\circ}
both in
\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes M_{\alpha_{ki}\times\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}}\subset\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}
. Then
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}}=\langle\xi_{i},\xi^{\prime}_{i}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}}\langle\eta_{j},\eta^{\prime}_{j}\rangle_{\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}}}\tr(u(v,w)^{\ast}u(v^{\prime},w))
where the trace factor is obtained from the identification of
M_{\alpha_{ki}\times\alpha_{\ell j}}
with
\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{ki}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\alpha_{\ell j}}
and we have used the fact that
\lambda(v)=\lambda(v^{\prime})=n_{i}
and
\rho(v)=\rho(v^{\prime})=n_{j}
to write the scalar products. This implies the formula in terms of the scalar product on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
, from which we deduce the relations on the norm in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
and on the scalar product in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
. This last relation implies the norm relation
\lVert\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v})\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\lVert\psi_{v}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}\Big(\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\lVert u(v,w)\rVert^{2}_{F}\Big)
. Then, suppose
\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\lVert u(v,w)\rVert^{2}_{F}>0
: if
\psi_{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
is such that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v})=0
, then
\lVert\psi_{v}\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}=0
, so that
\psi_{v}=0
, that is,
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}
is one-to-one. Suppose
\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\lVert u(v,w)\rVert^{2}_{F}=0
, then
\lVert\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v})\rVert^{2}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=0
for any
\psi_{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
, so that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}=0
, that is,
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}
is not one-to-one.

Notice that the condition
\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\lVert u(v,w)\rVert^{2}_{F}>0
for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
does not implies that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is one-to-one: one can consider a situation where, for
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
such that
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v)=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v^{\prime})
,
\psi_{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}
, and
\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v^{\prime}}
,
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\psi_{v})+\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v^{\prime}})=0\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
for some
w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
.

From (
15.1.8
), it is natural to define, for any
v,v^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\begin{cases}0&\text{if $\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{1})\neq\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{2})$}\\
\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\tr(u(v_{1},w)^{\ast}u(v_{2},w))&\text{if $\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{1})=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{2})$}\end{cases}

so that (
15.1.8
) can be written as
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{2}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle\psi_{v_{1}},\iota_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v_{1}}}\,\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}}
.

With
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{1})=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{2})=(\mathbf{n}_{i},\mathbf{n}_{j})
, one has
\mathbf{T}^{v_{2},v_{1}}=\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\tr(u(v_{2},w)^{\ast}u(v_{1},w))=\tsum_{w\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\overline{\tr(u(v_{1},w)^{\ast}u(v_{2},w))}=\overline{\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}}}
, so that
(\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}})_{v_{1},v_{2}}
is a Hermitian matrix, so that this matrix can be diagonalized. Recall that the labels
v_{1},v_{2}
depends on the choices of the orthonormal bases
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}_{1\leq p\leq\mu_{ij}}
of the spaces
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
’s: this diagonalization (see proof of Prop.
15.1.19
) is related to a change of these bases. This leads us to introduce the following Hypothesis.

Hypothesis 15.1.18
.

We suppose that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is one-to-one and is such that there are orthonormal bases
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}_{1\leq p\leq\mu_{ij}}
of the spaces
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
which conform to Prop.
4.2.9
(in the odd case) or Prop.
4.2.10
(in the even case), and such that, for the decomposition of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
induced by these bases,
\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}}=\mathbf{t}_{v_{1}}\delta^{v_{1},v_{2}}
when
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{1})=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{2})
, with real numbers
\mathbf{t}_{v}
such that
\mathbf{t}_{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}=\mathbf{t}_{v}
.

A direct consequence of these assumptions is that
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{2}}(\psi^{\prime}_{v_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=0
for any
v_{1}\neq v_{2}
and
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi^{\prime}_{v})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\mathbf{t}_{v}\,\langle\psi_{v},\psi^{\prime}_{v}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}
for any
v
. The one-to-one requirement is natural in the context of
AF
-algebras, since it generalizes the one-to-one requirement on
\phi
. On the other hand, the diagonalization requirement is not mandatory for the formal developments to come, but it will be useful to compare spectral actions for
\phi
-compatible spectral triples on
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
in Sect.
15.2
. Moreover, this requirement is satisfied for some
KO
-dimensions:

Proposition 15.1.19
.

Suppose that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
, and, in the even case, that
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
. Then, in
KO
-dimensions
0,1,2,6,7
, the diagonalization requirement in Hypothesis
15.1.18
is satisfied for any
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
.

Proof

Let
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}_{1\leq p\leq\mu_{ij}}
be orthonormal bases of the spaces
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
which satisfy Prop.
4.2.9
(in the odd case) or Prop.
4.2.10
(in the even case). Let us first complete the notations introduced before Prop.
15.1.17
, where we have introduced the identification
v=(i,p,j)
. With this notation, we define
\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)=(j,\bar{p},i)
where
\bar{p}=1,\dots,\mu_{ji}=\mu_{ij}
and
\bar{\bar{p}}=p
(obviously, this bar is not to be confused with a complex conjugation).

Let
\{\tau_{k\ell}^{q}\}_{1\leq q\leq\nu_{k\ell}}
be orthonormal bases of the spaces
\mathbb{C}^{\nu_{k\ell}}
where
\nu_{k\ell}
are the multiplicity of the irreps
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},{\mathbf{m}}_{k}{\mathbf{m}}_{\ell}}
in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
. These bases define the irreps
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
for
w=(k,q,\ell)\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B},{\mathbf{m}}_{k}{\mathbf{m}}_{\ell}}^{(0)}
as in (
4.2.1
). We have written the map
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w}
in terms of a matrix
u(v,w)
. It is convenient to write
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}
explicitly in terms of the bases
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}
and
\{\tau_{k\ell}^{q}\}
. In order to avoid cumbersome notations, we use the identification
\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{n_{i}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{n_{j}\circ}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
(resp.
\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\nu_{k\ell}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}\simeq\mathbb{C}^{m_{k}}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{m_{\ell}\circ}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{\nu_{k\ell}}
) so that
\sigma_{ij}^{p}
(resp.
\tau_{k\ell}^{q}
) will appear on the right in the tensor products. Then we can replace the notation
u(v,w)
by the notation
u_{k\ell,q}^{ij,p}\in M_{\alpha_{ki}\times\alpha_{\ell j}}
which refers to the bases
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}
and
\{\tau_{k\ell}^{q}\}
for which, similarly to (
15.1.5
), one has
\phi_{\mathcal{H},w}^{v}(\xi_{i}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\otimes\sigma_{ij}^{p})=I_{k,\ell}^{i,j}\big(\xi_{i}\otimes u_{k\ell,q}^{ij,p}\otimes\eta_{j}^{\circ}\big)\otimes\tau_{k\ell}^{q}
(no summation). In the
p
and
q
variables,
\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}}
with
\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{1})=\pi_{\lambda\rho}(v_{2})=(\mathbf{n}_{i},\mathbf{n}_{j})
then becomes
\mathbf{T}^{p_{1},p_{2}}_{ij}=\tsum_{k,\ell,q}\tr((u^{ij,p_{2}}_{k\ell,q})^{\ast}u^{ij,p_{1}}_{k\ell,q})
for
p_{1},p_{2}=1,\dots,\mu_{ij}
. Notice the switch
1\leftrightarrow 2
which will be convenient later. Since we suppose that
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
, by Prop.
15.1.15
one get (
15.1.7
) in terms of the new notations:
u_{\ell k,\bar{q}}^{ji,\bar{p}}=\tfrac{\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p,j,d_{\mathcal{A}})}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}(k,\ell,q,d_{\mathcal{B}})}(u_{k\ell,q}^{ij,p})^{\ast}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{\bar{p}_{1},\bar{p}_{2}}_{ji}

\displaystyle=\tsum_{\ell,k,\bar{q}}\tr((u^{ji,\bar{p}_{2}}_{\ell k,\bar{q}})^{\ast}u^{ji,\bar{p}_{1}}_{\ell k,\bar{q}})=\tsum_{k,\ell,q}\tfrac{\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p_{2},j,d_{\mathcal{A}})\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p_{1},j,d_{\mathcal{A}})}{\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}(k,\ell,q,d_{\mathcal{B}})^{2}}\tr(u^{ij,p_{2}}_{k\ell,q}(u^{ij,p_{1}}_{k\ell,q})^{\ast})

\displaystyle=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p_{1},j,d_{\mathcal{A}})\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p_{2},j,d_{\mathcal{A}})\mathbf{T}^{p_{2},p_{1}}_{ij}

(15.1.9)

In the following, we fix the couple
(i,j)
. Let us introduce a change of bases
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}
to
\{\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ij}\}
in
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
, where
\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ij}=\tsum_{p}u^{p^{\prime}p}\sigma_{ij}^{p}
for a unitary matrix
U=(u^{p^{\prime}p})_{p^{\prime},p}
. Then a straightforward computation shows that the matrices
u^{\prime ij,p^{\prime}}_{k\ell,q}
defined relatively to the bases
\{\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ij}\}
and
\{\tau_{k\ell}^{q}\}
are
u^{\prime ij,p^{\prime}}_{k\ell,q}=\tsum_{p}u^{p^{\prime}p}u^{ij,p}_{k\ell,q}
, and the associated
\mathbf{T}^{\prime p^{\prime}_{1},p^{\prime}_{2}}_{ij}=\tsum_{k,\ell,q}\tr((u^{\prime ij,p^{\prime}_{2}}_{k\ell,q})^{\ast}u^{\prime ij,p^{\prime}_{1}}_{k\ell,q})
become

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{\prime p^{\prime}_{1},p^{\prime}_{2}}_{ij}

\displaystyle=\tsum_{k,\ell,q}\tsum_{p_{1},p_{2}}\overline{u^{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{2}}}u^{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\,\tr((u^{ij,p_{2}}_{k\ell,q})^{\ast}u^{ij,p_{1}}_{k\ell,q})=\tsum_{p_{1},p_{2}}\overline{u^{p^{\prime}_{2}p_{2}}}u^{p^{\prime}_{1}p_{1}}\,\mathbf{T}^{p_{1},p_{2}}_{ij},

so that
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ij}=U\mathbf{T}_{ij}U^{\ast}
with
U^{\ast}=(\overline{u^{pp^{\prime}}})_{p^{\prime},p}
(here we use the switch
1\leftrightarrow 2
mentioned before). Since
\mathbf{T}_{ij}
is a Hermitian matrix, there is a unitary matrix
U
such that
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ij}=U\mathbf{T}_{ij}U^{\ast}
is diagonal with real eigenvalues
\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{p}=\mathbf{t}_{v}
. So, for the new basis
\{\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ij}\}
of
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
defined by
U
,
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ij}
, and so
(\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}})_{v_{1},v_{2}}
, is diagonal.

Let us now look how this diagonalization can be performed according to the constraints given in proposition
4.2.9
(in the odd case) or Prop.
4.2.10
(in the even case). The first constraint, common to Prop.
4.2.9
and
4.2.10
, is
\sigma_{ji}^{\bar{p}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p,j,d_{\mathcal{A}})L_{ij}(\overline{\sigma}_{ij}^{p})
for any
p
, where
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p,j,d_{\mathcal{A}})=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(v,d_{\mathcal{A}})
is defined in (
4.2.2
).

Let us first consider the case
i<j
(for any
KO
-dimension), for which
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p,j,d_{\mathcal{A}})=1
and
\bar{p}=p
, so that, from (
15.1.9
), one has
\mathbf{T}^{p_{1},p_{2}}_{ji}=\mathbf{T}^{p_{2},p_{1}}_{ij}
:
\mathbf{T}_{ji}
is the transpose of
\mathbf{T}_{ij}
. Since this result is true in any basis of
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
, this implies
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ji}=\bar{U}\mathbf{T}_{ji}\bar{U}^{\ast}
. On the other hand,
\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ji}=L_{ij}(\overline{\sigma}^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ij})=\tsum_{p}\overline{u^{p^{\prime}p}}L_{ij}(\overline{\sigma}_{ij}^{p})=\tsum_{p}\overline{u^{p^{\prime}p}}\sigma^{p}_{ji}
, so that the change of bases from
\{\sigma^{p}_{ji}\}
to
\{\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ji}\}
in
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ji}}
is performed by the unitary matrix
\bar{U}
. From these two compatible relations, one concludes that the change of basis defined by
U
in
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
which diagonalizes
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ij}
automatically induces a change of bases
\bar{U}
in
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ji}}
which diagonalizes
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ji}
. Notice then that the eigenvalues
\mathbf{t}_{ji}^{p}
in
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ji}
are the same as the eigenvalues
\mathbf{t}_{ij}^{p}
in
\mathbf{T}^{\prime}_{ij}
, so that
\mathbf{t}_{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}=\mathbf{t}_{v}
.

Let us now consider
i=j
in
KO
-dimensions
0,1,7
. Then, as before,
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,p,i,d_{\mathcal{A}})=1
and
\bar{p}=p
, so that, from (
15.1.9
), one has
\mathbf{T}^{p_{1},p_{2}}_{ii}=\mathbf{T}^{p_{2},p_{1}}_{ii}
, and we already know that
\mathbf{T}^{p_{1},p_{2}}_{ii}=\overline{\mathbf{T}^{p_{2},p_{1}}_{ii}}
: the matrix
\mathbf{T}_{ii}
is a real symmetric matrix, and the diagonalizing matrix
U
can be chosen to be an orthogonal matrix (so a real matrix). This result is compatible with the required condition
\sigma_{ii}^{p}=L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}_{ii}^{p})
on the basis since
\sigma^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ii}=L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}^{\prime p^{\prime}}_{ii})=\tsum_{p}\overline{u^{p^{\prime}p}}L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}_{ii}^{p})=\tsum_{p}\overline{u^{p^{\prime}p}}\sigma_{ii}^{p}=\tsum_{p}u^{p^{\prime}p}\sigma_{ii}^{p}
. Here, it is trivial that
\mathbf{t}_{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}=\mathbf{t}_{v}
since
\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)=v
.

Finally, consider
i=j
in
KO
-dimensions
2,3,4,5,6
. In that situation, if
p=2a
(resp.
p=2a-1
) then
\bar{p}=2a-1
(resp.
\bar{p}=2a
), and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,2a-1,i,d_{\mathcal{A}})=1
and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}(i,2a,i,d_{\mathcal{A}})=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}
. The matrix
\mathbf{T}_{ii}
is a block matrix
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}&\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}\\
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}&\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,o}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
where
o
and
e
stand for odd and even: for instance
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}=\left(\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{1},2a_{2}}\right)
and
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}=\left(\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{1},2a_{2}-1}\right)
with
a_{1},a_{2}=1,\dots,\mu_{ii}/2
. Then, from (
15.1.9
), one has
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{1},2a_{2}}=\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{2}-1,2a_{1}-1}
,
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{1},2a_{2}-1}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{2},2a_{1}-1}
, and
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{1}-1,2a_{2}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{2a_{2}-1,2a_{1}}
. Considering these block matrices as matrices indexed by
a_{1},a_{2}
, this means that
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}={\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,o}}^{\top}
,
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}}^{\top}
, and
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}{\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}}^{\top}
. Since
\mathbf{T}_{ii}
is Hermitian, one also has
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}={\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}}^{\ast}
and
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}={\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}}^{\ast}
.

In
KO
-dimensions
3,4,5
, one has
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}=-1
, so that
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}=-{\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}}^{\top}={\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}}^{\ast}
, which implies that
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}
and
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}
are antisymmetric matrices. We report the analysis for
KO
-dimensions
2,6
after the following considerations.

In the even case, since
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
, from Lemma
15.1.11
,
u(v,w)
is non-zero only when
s(v)=s(w)
, so that the sum defining
\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}}
implies
s(w)=s(v_{1})=s(v_{2})
. The matrix
(\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}})_{v_{1},v_{2}}
is then block diagonal along the decomposition
s(v)=\pm 1
, and its diagonalization can be done by blocks: in terms of the change of bases in
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
, this means that the unitary
U
introduced above which diagonalizes
\mathbf{T}_{ij}
can be chosen to preserve the eigenspaces defined by the maps
\ell_{ij}
in Prop
4.2.10
. The decomposition along
s(v)=\pm 1
is preserved by
\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}
since
s_{ji}^{\bar{p}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}s_{ij}^{p}
: so all the previous developments are compatible with this choice for
U
.

In the case
i=j
and
KO
-dimensions
2,6
, from Prop.
4.2.10
, one has
s(i,2a,i)=1
and
s(i,2a-1,i)=-1
, so that the block decomposition
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}&\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}\\
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}&\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,o}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
corresponds to the block decomposition along
s(v)=\pm 1
, and from the previous considerations, this implies that
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,o}=\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,e}=0
. Since
\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}
is Hermitean, there is a unitary matrix
\widetilde{U}
such that
\widetilde{U}\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{e,e}\widetilde{U}^{\ast}
is diagonal, and then by transposition,
\overline{\widetilde{U}}\mathbf{T}_{ii}^{o,o}\overline{\widetilde{U}}^{\ast}
is also diagonal with the same eigenvalues, that is,
\mathbf{t}_{\kappa_{\mathcal{A}}(v)}=\mathbf{t}_{v}
. The unitary
U=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\widetilde{U}&0\\
0&\overline{\widetilde{U}}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
diagonalizes
\mathbf{T}_{ii}
and this diagonalization is compatible with the required conditions
\sigma_{ii}^{2a}=L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}_{ii}^{2a-1})
and
\sigma_{ii}^{2a-1}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}_{ii}^{2a})
:
\widetilde{U}=(\tilde{u}^{a^{\prime},a})
(resp.
\overline{\widetilde{U}}=(\bar{\tilde{u}}^{a^{\prime},a})
) induces a change of the sub-basis
\{\sigma_{ii}^{2a}\}
to
\{\sigma^{\prime 2a}_{ii}\}
(resp.
\{\sigma_{ii}^{2a-1}\}
to
\{\sigma^{\prime 2a-1}_{ii}\}
) with
\sigma^{\prime 2a^{\prime}}_{ii}=\tsum_{a}\tilde{u}^{a^{\prime},a}\sigma_{ii}^{2a}
(resp.
\sigma^{\prime 2a^{\prime}-1}_{ii}=\tsum_{a}\bar{\tilde{u}}^{a^{\prime},a}\sigma_{ii}^{2a-1}
). The required condition is satisfied since then
\sigma^{\prime 2a^{\prime}-1}_{ii}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}^{\prime 2a^{\prime}}_{ii})=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}\tsum_{a}\bar{\tilde{u}}^{a^{\prime},a}L_{ii}(\overline{\sigma}^{2a}_{ii})=\tsum_{a}\bar{\tilde{u}}^{a^{\prime},a}\sigma_{ii}^{2a-1}
.

We suspect that the diagonalization property proved in Prop.
15.1.19
could be true also in
KO
-dimensions
3,4,5
