. But we were unable to prove this fact. Nevertheless, the proposition fortunately covers the
KO
-dimension
6
used in the finite part of the spectral triple for the NC version of the Standard Model of Particles Physics, see
(
Chamseddine et al., 2007a
)
and
(
van Suijlekom, 2015
)
for instance.

Proposition 15.1.20
.

If two (odd/even) real spectral triples are
\phi
-compatible and
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is such that (
15.1.7
) holds, then they have the same
KO
-dimension (mod 8).

Proof

Since
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
satisfies (
15.1.7
), by Prop.
15.1.15
,
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
and is diagonal. By Lemma
15.1.5
,
\gamma_{\mathcal{B}}
is strong
\phi
-compatible with
\gamma_{\mathcal{A}}
and is diagonal. The difference with Prop.
15.1.6
, is that
D_{\mathcal{B}}
is only
\phi
-compatible with
D_{\mathcal{A}}
. So, we already get
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime\prime}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime\prime}
: it remains to consider
\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
and
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}
.

Since
J_{\mathcal{B}}
is diagonal, one has
J_{\mathcal{B}}D_{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}&J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}\\
J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}&J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
D_{\mathcal{B}}J_{\mathcal{B}}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}&D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\perp}J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}\\
D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\phi}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}&D_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}J_{\mathcal{B},\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, so that
J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}J_{\mathcal{B},\phi}^{\phi}
. Inserting this relation in the
\phi
-compatibility conditions on
J_{\mathcal{B}}
and
D_{\mathcal{B}}
implies
\epsilon_{\mathcal{B}}^{\prime}=\epsilon_{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
.

From Prop.
15.1.12
, the strong
\phi
-compatibility condition between
D_{\mathcal{B}}
and
D_{\mathcal{A}}
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\tsum_{v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(D_{\mathcal{A},(v_{1},v_{2})}\psi_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\tsum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}D_{\mathcal{B},(w_{1},w_{2})}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{1}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})

for any
v_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
,
w_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
, and
\psi_{v_{1}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v_{1}}
, and the
\phi
-compatibility condition is equivalent to

\displaystyle\tsum_{v_{2}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{2}}^{v_{2}}(D_{\mathcal{A},(v_{1},v_{2})}\psi_{v_{1}})

\displaystyle=\tsum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi,(w_{1},w_{2})}^{\phi}\phi_{\mathcal{H},w_{1}}^{v_{1}}(\psi_{v_{1}})

where
D_{\mathcal{B},\phi,(w_{1},w_{2})}^{\phi}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w_{1}}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w_{2}}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
. Unfortunately, from this relation, we cannot define the elementary operators
D_{\mathcal{B},\phi,(w_{1},w_{2})}^{\phi}
in terms of the elementary operators
D_{\mathcal{A},(v_{1},v_{2})}
. Only the operators
\tsum_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}D_{\mathcal{B},\phi,(w_{1},w_{2})}^{\phi}:\toplus_{w_{1}\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w_{1}}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B},w_{2}}\cap\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
can be recovered from the
D_{\mathcal{A},(v_{1},v_{2})}
’s.

15.2
Spectral Actions for
AF
-AC Manifolds

Given a spectral action
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}},\gamma_{\mathcal{A}})
for a finite dimensional algebra
\mathcal{A}
and given a Riemannian spin manifold
(M,g)
equipped with its canonical spectral triple
(C^{\infty}(M),L^{2}(S),D_{M},J_{M},\gamma_{M})
, we consider the spectral triple
(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\vcentcolon=C^{\infty}(M)\otimes\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=L^{2}(S)\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=D_{M}\otimes{\text{\char 49\relax}}+\gamma_{M}\otimes D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=J_{M}\otimes J_{\mathcal{A}},\gamma_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=\gamma_{M}\otimes\gamma_{\mathcal{A}})
over the Almost Commutative algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
. Obviously, this makes sense only when the
KO
-dimension for
M
and
\mathcal{A}
produces such a spectral triple, see for instance
(
Dąbrowski and Dossena, 2011
)
.

Then, given two spectral triples
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}},D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\mathcal{A}},\gamma_{\mathcal{A}})
and
(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}},D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\mathcal{B}},\gamma_{\mathcal{B}})
for two finite dimensional algebras
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, and a one-to-one morphism
\phi:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}
such that the two spectral triples are
\phi
-compatible, with
J_{\mathcal{B}}
strong
\phi
-compatible with
J_{\mathcal{A}}
, the aim of this section is to compare the spectral actions on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
(for the same Riemannian spin manifold
(M,g)
).

In order to have a good physical interpretation of the
\phi
-compatibility, in particular at the level of the fermions, we first need to introduce a “normalized”
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
map.

15.2.1
Normalized
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
Map

Denote by
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
a given morphism as in Def.
13.4.1
. We suppose that it satisfies Hypothesis
15.1.18
. Then we can choose the orthonormal bases
\{\sigma_{ij}^{p}\}_{1\leq p\leq\mu_{ij}}
of
\mathbb{C}^{\mu_{ij}}
that diagonalize
(\mathbf{T}^{v_{1},v_{2}})_{v_{1},v_{2}}
and this implies that
\{\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}(e_{\tilde{v}})\}_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}
is a basis of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
. For any
\tilde{v}=(v,\alpha)
, we can identify
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}(e_{\tilde{v}})
with
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(e_{\tilde{v}})
. Let
\tilde{v}_{1}=(v_{1},\alpha_{1})
and
\tilde{v}_{2}=(v_{2},\alpha_{2})
. When
v_{1}\neq v_{2}
, one has
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(e_{\tilde{v}_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(e_{\tilde{v}_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=0
, while, when
v=v_{1}=v_{2}
,
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(e_{\tilde{v}_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(e_{\tilde{v}_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\mathbf{t}_{v}\,\langle\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}=\mathbf{t}_{v}\,\delta_{\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}}
. This implies that
\{\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}(e_{\tilde{v}})\}_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}
is an orthogonal family. Since
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}
is one-to-one and
\lVert\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(e_{\tilde{v}})\rVert^{2}=\mathbf{t}_{v}
, one has
\mathbf{t}_{v}\neq 0
for any
v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
.

Definition 15.2.1
.

The normalized
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
map associated to the map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
which satisfies Hypothesis
15.1.18
is the map
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
defined by

\displaystyle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\toplus_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\psi_{v})\vcentcolon=\tsum_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\mathbf{t}_{v(\tilde{v})}^{-1/2}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0,v}(\psi_{v})

(15.2.1)

Using (
15.1.1
) with
b_{j}=0
, one can check that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
satisfies Def.
13.4.1
.

The normalization has been chosen such that the family
\{f_{\tilde{v}}\vcentcolon=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(e_{\tilde{v}})\}_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}
is an orthonormal basis of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
. This basis of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
can be completed with any orthonormal basis
\{f_{\hat{w}}\}_{\hat{w}\in\widehat{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}
of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
where
\widehat{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}
is any index set for this basis. So,
\{f_{\tilde{v}}\}_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\cup\{f_{\hat{w}}\}_{\hat{w}\in\widehat{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}
is an orthonormal basis of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
adapted to the decomposition
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})\oplus\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
.

We now consider the normalized
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
map in place of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{0}
. The relation between the scalar products in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
reduces to the simple relation
\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi^{\prime}_{v})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle\psi_{v},\psi^{\prime}_{v}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A},v}}
for any
v
.

In the following,
\phi
-compatibility of operators will be relative to the normalized
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
map.

For an operator
B
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
which is
\phi
-compatible with an operator
A
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, the components
B_{\phi}^{\perp}
,
B_{\perp}^{\phi}
, and
B_{\perp}^{\perp}
of
B
in the
2\times 2
matrix decomposition induced by
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}=\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})\oplus\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
will be called non-inherited, while the component
B_{\phi}^{\phi}
will be called inherited. Let us use the acronym “
TNIC
” for “Terms with Non-Inherited Components” in the following technical results, which are the main interest for the use of the normalized
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
map:

Lemma 15.2.2
.

For
i=1,\ldots,n
, let
B_{i}
be an operator on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
which is
\phi
-compatible with an operator
A_{i}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

1.

For any
\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
, one has:
\qquad\qquad\langle f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},B_{1}\cdots B_{n}f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle e_{\tilde{v}_{1}},A_{1}\cdots A_{n}e_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}

2.

As a consequence, one has:
\quad\qquad\qquad\tr(B_{1}\cdots B_{n})=\tr(A_{1}\cdots A_{n})+\text{{\small TNIC}}

Proof

First, let us prove the relation in Point
1
for
n=1
. We omit the index
i
. Using the matrix decomposition
Ae_{\tilde{v}}=\tsum_{\tilde{v}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}A_{\tilde{v}}^{\tilde{v}^{\prime}}e_{\tilde{v}^{\prime}}
along the basis
\{e_{\tilde{v}}\}_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}
, the RHS is
A_{\tilde{v}_{2}}^{\tilde{v}_{1}}
. For the LHS, one has
\langle f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},Bf_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(e_{\tilde{v}_{1}}),B_{\phi}^{\phi}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{2}}(e_{\tilde{v}_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(e_{\tilde{v}_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{2}}(Ae_{\tilde{v}_{2}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\tsum_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}A_{\tilde{v}_{2}}^{\tilde{v}}\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(e_{\tilde{v}_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(e_{\tilde{v}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}
. This expression is zero for
v_{1}\neq v
, so the summation reduces to the summation over the
\tilde{v}=(v_{1},\alpha)\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
:
\tsum_{\tilde{v}=(v_{1},\alpha)}A_{\tilde{v}_{2}}^{\tilde{v}}\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(e_{\tilde{v}_{1}}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v_{1}}(e_{\tilde{v}})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\tsum_{\tilde{v}=(v_{1},\alpha)}A_{\tilde{v}_{2}}^{\tilde{v}}\langle e_{\tilde{v}_{1}},e_{\tilde{v}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}=A_{\tilde{v}_{2}}^{\tilde{v}_{1}}
.

Let us return to the general situation
n\geq 1
in Point
1
. With
B_{i}=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}B_{i,\phi}^{\phi}&B_{i,\phi}^{\perp}\\
B_{i,\perp}^{\phi}&B_{i,\perp}^{\perp}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, a straightforward computation shows that the only component in
B_{1}\cdots B_{n}
that contains only inherited components is in the block
(B_{1}\cdots B_{n})_{\phi}^{\phi}
and it is
B_{1,\phi}^{\phi}\cdots B_{n,\phi}^{\phi}
, so that
\langle f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},B_{1}\cdots B_{n}f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},B_{1,\phi}^{\phi}\cdots B_{n,\phi}^{\phi}f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}
. The proof that
\langle f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},B_{1,\phi}^{\phi}\cdots B_{n,\phi}^{\phi}f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\langle e_{\tilde{v}_{1}},A_{1}\cdots A_{n}e_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}
is the same as before, with
B_{\phi}^{\phi}=B_{1,\phi}^{\phi}\cdots B_{n,\phi}^{\phi}
and
A=A_{1}\cdots A_{n}
which satisfy
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(A\psi)=B_{\phi}^{\phi}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
.

Point
2
is a direct consequence of Point
1
. By the previous argument on the product
B_{1}\cdots B_{n}
, one has

\displaystyle\tr(B_{1}\cdots B_{n})

\displaystyle=\tsum_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\langle f_{\tilde{v}},B_{1}\cdots B_{n}f_{\tilde{v}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}+\tsum_{\hat{w}\in\widehat{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{B}}^{(0)}}\langle f_{\hat{w}},B_{1}\cdots B_{n}f_{\hat{w}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\tsum_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\langle f_{\tilde{v}},B_{1}\cdots B_{n}f_{\tilde{v}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}

and
\tsum_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\langle f_{\tilde{v}},B_{1}\cdots B_{n}f_{\tilde{v}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\tsum_{\tilde{v}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\langle e_{\tilde{v}},A_{1}\cdots A_{n}e_{\tilde{v}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}=\tr(A_{1}\cdots A_{n})+\text{{\small TNIC}}
by Point
1
.

Remark 15.2.3
.

Point
2
can be proved directly without the assumption that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is normalized.

The notion of
\phi
-compatibility has been developed for operators on
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
. We define
\phi
-compatibility for fermions as follows:

Definition 15.2.4
.

A vector
\psi_{\mathcal{B}}
is
\phi
-compatible with a vector
\psi_{\mathcal{A}}
if
\psi_{\mathcal{B}}-\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi_{\mathcal{A}})\in\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})^{\perp}
.

Using this definition, we extend the acronym “
TNIC
” (“Terms with Non-Inherited Components”) to terms which contains fermions.

From a physical point of view, the consequence of the normalization of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
is that, for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, one has

\displaystyle\lVert\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\lVert\psi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}

since, with
\psi=\toplus_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\psi_{v}
, one has
\lVert\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}^{2}=\toplus_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\langle\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v}),\phi_{\mathcal{H}}^{v}(\psi_{v})\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}}=\toplus_{v\in\Gamma_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}}\langle\psi_{v},\psi_{v}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}=\lVert\psi\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}}^{2}
. This means that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
respects the normalization of the state vector when it is injected from
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
into
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
. This state
\psi
can be “diluted” at different “places” in
\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
(different irreps) but its norm is conserved.

15.2.2
Comparison of Spectral Actions

The purpose of this section is to compare the spectral actions for the two
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible spectral triples

\displaystyle(\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=D_{M}\otimes{\text{\char 49\relax}}+\gamma_{M}\otimes D_{\mathcal{A}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=J_{M}\otimes J_{\mathcal{A}},\gamma_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\vcentcolon=\gamma_{M}\otimes\gamma_{\mathcal{A}})

and

\displaystyle(\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\vcentcolon=D_{M}\otimes{\text{\char 49\relax}}+\gamma_{M}\otimes D_{\mathcal{B}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\vcentcolon=J_{M}\otimes J_{\mathcal{B}},\gamma_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\vcentcolon=\gamma_{M}\otimes\gamma_{\mathcal{B}})

for the same Riemannian spin manifold
(M,g)
. We suppose that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{B}}
is normalized.

We extend in a natural way the map
\phi
to a morphism of algebras
\widehat{\phi}\vcentcolon=\Id\otimes\phi:\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\to\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
. In the same way, we denote by
\widehat{\phi}_{\mathcal{H}}\vcentcolon=\Id\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{H}}:\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\to\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}
the natural extension of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
. The notion of (strong)
\widehat{\phi}
-compatibility is then naturally defined from the notion of (strong)
\phi
-compatibility: an operator
\hat{B}=B_{M}\otimes B_{F}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}
is (strong)
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible with an operator
\hat{A}=A_{M}\otimes A_{F}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
if
\widehat{\phi}_{\mathcal{H}}(\hat{A}(\chi\otimes\psi))=\hat{B}_{\widehat{\phi}}^{\widehat{\phi}}\widehat{\phi}_{\mathcal{H}}(\chi\otimes\psi)
(resp.
\widehat{\phi}_{\mathcal{H}}(\hat{A}\chi\otimes\psi)=\hat{B}\widehat{\phi}_{\mathcal{H}}(\chi\otimes\psi)
) for any
\chi\otimes\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
, that is,
\chi\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(A_{F}\psi)=\chi\otimes B_{F,\phi}^{\phi}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
(resp.
\chi\otimes\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(A_{F}\psi)=\chi\otimes B_{F}\phi_{\mathcal{H}}(\psi)
).

Lemma
15.2.2
then extends naturally to:

Lemma 15.2.5
.

Let
\{\chi_{c}\}_{c\in C}
be an orthonormal basis of
L^{2}(S)
. For
i=1,\ldots,n
, let
\hat{B}_{i}
be an operator on
\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}
which is
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible with an operator
\hat{A}_{i}
on
\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
.

1.

For any
\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{\mathcal{A}}^{(0)}
and
c_{1},c_{2}\in C
, one has

\displaystyle\langle\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},\hat{B}_{1}\cdots\hat{B}_{n}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}

\displaystyle=\langle\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes e_{\tilde{v}_{1}},\hat{A}_{1}\cdots\hat{A}_{n}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes e_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}

2.

As a consequence, one has

\displaystyle\Tr(\hat{B}_{1}\cdots\hat{B}_{n})=\Tr(\hat{A}_{1}\cdots\hat{A}_{n})+\text{{\small TNIC}}

Proof

The proof is similar to the one of Lemma
15.2.2
, noticing that the geometrical part plays no role in the main steps.

We follow
(
van Suijlekom, 2015
)
to define the bosonic and the fermionic spectral actions. For any
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}})
, let us consider the operator
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}=D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}+\omega+\epsilon_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}^{\prime}J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\omega J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}^{-1}
where
\omega
is used in place of
\pi_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)
. Let
f:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a positive even function. Then the bosonic spectral action is defined by (
11.6.6
).

To define the fermionic spectral action, we introduce the vector space of Grassmann vectors
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
defined from
\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
, and the notation
\widetilde{\psi}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
. Then, in the even case, for any
\widetilde{\psi}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}^{+}
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}^{+}
corresponds to Grassmann vectors associated to vectors
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}^{+}=\ker(\gamma_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}-1)
(even elements in
\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
). Then the fermionic spectral action is defined by (
11.6.7
).

From now on, we suppose that
\dim M=4
and, to simplify the presentation (to focus mainly on the algebraic part of the spectral actions), we suppose that
(M,g)
is compact and flat, so that all the Riemann tensors will be trivial in the following.

Let us use the following notations. For any
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}})
with
\pi_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)=\gamma^{\mu}\otimes\tilde{A_{\mu}}+\gamma_{M}\otimes\tilde{\Phi}
, for Hermitian operators
\tilde{A_{\mu}}
and
\tilde{\Phi}
on
C^{\infty}(M)\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}}
, define
A_{\mu}\vcentcolon=\tilde{A_{\mu}}-J_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{A_{\mu}}J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
and
\Phi\vcentcolon=D_{\mathcal{A}}+\tilde{\Phi}+J_{\mathcal{A}}\tilde{\Phi}J_{\mathcal{A}}^{-1}
, so that
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}=D_{M}\otimes 1+\gamma^{\mu}\otimes A_{\mu}+\gamma_{M}\otimes\Phi
.

Let
\nabla_{\mu}^{S}
be the spin connection on
S
, and consider the vector bundle
E=S\otimes(M\times\mathcal{H}_{\mathcal{A}})
such that
L^{2}(E)=\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
, and let
\nabla_{\mu}^{E}\vcentcolon=\nabla_{\mu}^{S}\otimes 1+1\otimes(\partial_{\mu}+iA_{\mu})
be the natural twisted connection on
E
defined by the spectral triple, so that
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}=-i\gamma^{\mu}\nabla_{\mu}^{E}+\gamma_{M}\otimes\Phi
. Finally, let
D_{\mu}\vcentcolon=\partial_{\mu}+i\ad(A_{\mu})
and
F_{\mu\nu}\vcentcolon=\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}A_{\mu}+i[A_{\mu},A_{\nu}]
. In the same way, we introduce
\omega^{\prime}
,
\tilde{A}^{\prime}_{\mu}
,
\tilde{\Phi}^{\prime}
,
A^{\prime}_{\mu}
,
\Phi^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime}
,
D^{\prime}_{\mu}
,
F^{\prime}_{\mu\nu}
for the algebra
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
.

Let
f_{n}\vcentcolon=\int_{0}^{\infty}f(x)x^{n-1}\text{{d}}x
be the moments of
f
for
n>0
, then we have the general result
(
van Suijlekom, 2015
, Prop. 8.12)
that we have simplified to take into account the fact that the metric
g
is Euclidean. In particular there is no Einstein-Hilbert Lagrangian since the purely geometric part needs not be compared from
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
to
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proposition 15.2.6
.

Suppose that the
KO
-dimension of
\mathcal{A}
is even, then

\displaystyle\Tr f(D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}/\Lambda)\sim\int_{M}\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu},\Phi)\,\text{{d}}^{4}x+\mathcal{O}(\Lambda^{-1})

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(A_{\mu},\Phi)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{A}(A_{\mu})+\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}(A_{\mu},\Phi)

where
\mathcal{L}_{A}(A_{\mu})=\frac{f(0)}{24\pi^{2}}\tr(F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu})
, and, up to a boundary term,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\Phi}(A_{\mu},\Phi)

\displaystyle=-\frac{2f_{2}\Lambda^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\tr(\Phi^{2})+\frac{f(0)}{8\pi^{2}}\tr(\Phi^{4})+\frac{f(0)}{8\pi^{2}}\tr\big((D_{\mu}\Phi)(D^{\mu}\Phi)\big)

We use the same function
f
and the same cut-off
\Lambda
for the spectral actions on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
. It corresponds to the same Lagrangian as in (
11.6.11
), without the terms coming from the metric of
M
.

We suppose that
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}})
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}})
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible in the sense that
\pi_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}(\omega^{\prime})
and
\pi_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible. Since the family of vectors
\{\gamma^{\mu},\gamma_{M}\}
are linearly independent in the Clifford algebra generated by the
\gamma^{\mu}
’s, this implies that
\tilde{A}^{\prime}_{\mu}
(resp.
\tilde{\Phi}^{\prime}
) is
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible with
\tilde{A}_{\mu}
(resp.
\tilde{\Phi}
). The strong
\phi
-compatibility between
J_{\mathcal{B}}
and
J_{\mathcal{A}}
then implies that
A^{\prime}_{\mu}
(resp.
\Phi^{\prime}
) is
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible with
A_{\mu}
(resp.
\Phi
). Notice then that
\partial_{\mu}\Phi^{\prime}
(resp.
\partial_{\mu}A^{\prime}_{\nu}
) is
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible with
\partial_{\mu}\Phi
(resp.
\partial_{\mu}A_{\nu}
).
2
2

2

Thanks to the fact that
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
does not depend on the points in
M
.
We then have:

Proposition 15.2.7
.

Suppose that
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}})
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}})
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible in the previous sense. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},A}(A^{\prime}_{\mu})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},A}(A_{\mu})+\text{{\small TNIC}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\Phi^{\prime}}(A^{\prime}_{\mu},\Phi^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\Phi}(A_{\mu},\Phi)+\text{{\small TNIC}}

Proof

From Prop.
15.2.6
, all the terms in
\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},A}(A^{\prime}_{\mu})
and
\mathcal{L}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\Phi^{\prime}}(A^{\prime}_{\mu},\Phi^{\prime})
are traces of polynomials of
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible elements. So, according to Lemma
15.2.5
, up to terms with non-inherited components, they are equal to the similar expression in terms of traces of polynomials of the corresponding elements on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
.

Remark 15.2.8
.

This Proposition can be proved without the assumption on the normalization of
\phi_{\mathcal{H}}
, see Remark
15.2.3
.

A slight extension of Prop.
15.1.8
shows that
\omega\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}})
and
\omega^{\prime}\vcentcolon=\widehat{\phi}(\omega)\in\Omega^{1}_{U}(\widehat{\mathcal{B}})
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible. But then
\omega^{\prime}
contains only inherited degrees of freedom, and so this situation is quite trivial from a physical point of view.

Proposition 15.2.9
.

If
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}
is
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible with
\widetilde{\psi}
, then

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},f}[\omega^{\prime},\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}]

\displaystyle=\langle J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}=\langle J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\widetilde{\psi},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}\widetilde{\psi}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}=\mathcal{S}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},f}[\omega,\widetilde{\psi}]+\text{{\small TNIC}}

Proof

Since
\pi_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}(\omega^{\prime})
and
\pi_{D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}(\omega)
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible,
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}
and
D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible, and since
J_{\mathcal{B}}
and
J_{\mathcal{A}}
are strong
\phi
-compatible, then
J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}
and
J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
are strong
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible.

Using previously defined notations, one can write
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}=\tsum_{c,\tilde{v}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c,\tilde{v}}\chi_{c}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}}+\tsum_{c,\hat{w}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c,\tilde{v}}\chi_{c}\otimes f_{\hat{w}}
and
\widetilde{\psi}=\tsum_{c,\tilde{v}}\widetilde{\psi}^{c,\tilde{v}}\chi_{c}\otimes e_{\tilde{v}}
. Since
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{\psi}
are
\widehat{\phi}
-compatible, one has
\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c,\tilde{v}}=\widetilde{\psi}^{c,\tilde{v}}
for any
c,\tilde{v}
. So,
\langle J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}=\tsum_{c_{1},c_{2},\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c_{1},\tilde{v}_{1}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c_{2},\tilde{v}_{2}}\langle J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}=\epsilon_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\tsum_{c_{1},c_{2},\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c_{1},\tilde{v}_{1}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime c_{2},\tilde{v}_{2}}\overline{\langle\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}}
. From Lemma
15.2.5
, one has
\langle\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}=\langle\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes e_{\tilde{v}_{1}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes e_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}
so that, since
\epsilon_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}=\epsilon_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}
,
\langle J_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}},\omega^{\prime}}\widetilde{\psi}^{\prime}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}=\epsilon_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\tsum_{c_{1},c_{2},\tilde{v}_{1},\tilde{v}_{2}}\widetilde{\psi}^{c_{1},\tilde{v}_{1}}\widetilde{\psi}^{c_{2},\tilde{v}_{2}}\overline{\langle\chi_{c_{1}}\otimes f_{\tilde{v}_{1}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}\chi_{c_{2}}\otimes e_{\tilde{v}_{2}}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}=\langle J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}\widetilde{\psi},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}},\omega}\widetilde{\psi}\rangle_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}}}+\text{{\small TNIC}}
.

Remark 15.2.10
.

Notice that the formal proofs presented in the previous Propositions, which compare the spectral action on
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}
to the spectral action on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
, do not reveal the terms which mix inherited and non-inherited components. A concrete and complete computation is necessary to compare precisely the two Lagrangians. This computation was done but is too long to be presented here.

These results can be collected to construct a sequence
\{(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n},\mathcal{H}_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}},D_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}},J_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}},\gamma_{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}})\}_{n\geq 0}
) of even real spectral triples (on AC manifolds) which are
\widehat{\phi}_{n,n+1}
-compatible and a sequence of their corresponding spectral actions
\mathcal{S}_{b}[\omega_{n}]+\mathcal{S}_{f}[\omega_{n},\widetilde{\psi}_{n}]
with a control about their inherited and non-inherited terms. Using slight modifications of Prop.
15.1.9
and
15.1.14
, a gauge transformation on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n}
can be transported to a gauge transformation on
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{n+1}
. So, we end up with a sequence of compatible NCGFT constructed on top of the defining sequence of an
AF
-algebra
\mathcal{A}=\varinjlim\mathcal{A}_{n}
.

Chapter 16
Conclusions and Outlooks

In this chapter, we propose to conclude this part and therefore the essential results put forward in this thesis, thus opening some perspectives for future works.

Both frameworks allow us to find the Lagrangian on top of
\mathcal{A}
into the one on top of
\mathcal{B}
. In the case of the derivations, this depends on the convention chosen to define the integration (see (
4.1.1
)). In the case of spectral triples, it depends on the choice of taking the
\phi
-normalized compatibility (see (
15.2.1
)). These two choices are linked to how a “scalar weight” is associated with the objects used. The fact that the action of
\mathcal{A}
is recovered at the rank
\mathcal{B}
is an important indication of the naturalness of these constructions, particularly concerning the intuition (underlying the GUTs) of extending a theory by keeping some of its structures.

The derivation framework is more constrained by the formalism than the spectral triple one. From a mathematical point of view, this may seem to be an advantage as there are fewer free choices. But from the point of view of physical applications, the resulting structure is probably poorer than with the spectral triples where additional choices to the natural structure of the
AF
-algebra appear for each of the structural operators. However, as suggested in section
14.4
, a potential interesting phenomenology concerning the prediction of bosons masses should be explored.

The spectral triple framework offers the possibility to work with both notions of
\phi
-compatibility for the operators. It would be interesting to determine the results for the Lagrangian by taking the strong
\phi
-compatibility and/or any
\phi
-map (not necessarily the
\phi
-normalized). In the same way, a numerical exploration remains to be done in the case of spectral triples.

Unfortunately, the implemented project is very large and I didn’t get the time to explore all its relevant aspects. Then many questions remain to be explored, in particular, what is the tractability offered along the inductive sequence by the
\phi
-compatibility notions, by the norm or by Dirac operators? What is the meaning of the NCGFT at the end of the inductive sequence, given that it would potentially have an infinite number of degrees of freedom? Which
AF
-algebra can be interesting to make NCGFTs on it? Knowing that
AF
-algebras can approximate geometries and that our NCGFTs benefit from a geometrical interpretation, to what extent can the implemented scheme allow us to approximate geometries?

The two frameworks thus established offer a large number of possibilities concerning the elaboration of phenomenological models. Indeed, many choices are possible, concerning the
AF
-algebra, the module (or Hilbert space), the chosen notion of
\phi
-compatibility and of the structural operators for the spectral triplets, or the way
u(v,w)
embeds the fermionic fields, potentially allowing a “rotation" mechanism in the representation space (which could be linked to the CKM matrix mechanism). After the end of my PhD thesis, I hope to have the opportunity to work with researchers having some expertise in these fields, in order to study and explore GUT-like models beyond the NCSMPP, within this framework.

Finally, this work goes beyond the development of NCGFTs. Indeed, a large part of the efforts made during my PhD thesis have concerned the elaboration of differential structures on
AF
-algebras, with notions of compatibility between the operators allowing to build and relate these structures at each step of the sequence. There remain many avenues to explore from this simple fact, notably on the possibility of approximating interesting, potentially commutative geometries with this procedure.
